Chuck Lorre, ‘Grey’s Anatomy’ EP Zoanne Clack, ‘A Different World’ Boss Felicia Pride Join Lineup for SIE Society’s Impact+Profit26 (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In addition, entertainment-education pioneer Miguel Sabido has been tapped to receive the SIE Impact Entertainment Visionaries Award at the Dec. 7-8 conference, which will be held for the first time at USC Annenberg.

The SIE Society (Social Impact Entertainment Society) has unveiled the lineup for Impact+Profit26, its flagship conference “showing that storytellers don’t have to choose between impact and profit.”

TV uber-creator Chuck Lorre, Grey’s Anatomy EP Zoanne Clack and A Different World showrunner Felicia Pride are among those scheduled to take part in the two-day event, which is set for Dec. 7-8.

In addition, entertainment-education pioneer Miguel Sabido has been tapped to receive the SIE Impact Entertainment Visionaries Award at the conference, which will be held for the first time at USC Annenberg, home of the Hollywood, Health & Society, a program of the USC Norman Lear Center. Related Stories TV 'Grey's Anatomy' Texas Spinoff Casts Karla Souza in Lead Role TV Where to Watch 'Grey's Anatomy' Season 23 Online

Organizers said the new venue carries special meaning for the field. USC Annenberg’s connection to impact storytelling traces back to the pioneering Soap Summits of the 1990s and early 2000s, which helped spark television’s social impact movement and paved the way for Hollywood, Health & Society.

More than 250 producers, executives, financiers and changemakers are expected to attend the conference, which is presented for the fifth consecutive year in collaboration with the Johns Hopkins Center for Communication Programs.

“Bringing Impact+Profit to USC Annenberg connects us to the history of entertainment education in Hollywood,” said Will Nix, a founder and co-executive director of the SIE Society, whose creative work as a producer includes film, television, theater and digital media productions. “For four years, Johns Hopkins Center for Communication Programs has helped us bring evidence and storytelling into the same room. Impact+Profit is built on partnerships, and we’re grateful to every collaborator who makes it possible.”

Sabido will be honored on Day 1 of the event. The presentation will take place during “The Man Who Proved Stories Can Change the World: A Conversation With Miguel Sabido,” where Sabido will be joined onstage by Robert Rippberger, a founder and co-executive director of the SIE Society, filmmaker and author of The Power of Storytelling: Social Impact Entertainment.

“Long before anyone had a name for this field, Miguel showed the world that a story told with care and purpose could change how millions of people live their lives,” Rippberger said. “His work is the blueprint for what social impact entertainment can achieve. As we celebrate his legacy, we’re also challenging the next generation of storytellers to carry that vision forward with the same courage and rigor.”

The conference will close with “A Conversation With Chuck Lorre,” featuring the Golden Globe-winning writer and producer behind such comedy hits as The Big Bang Theory, Two and a Half Men, Mom, Young Sheldon and Georgie & Mandy’s First Marriage. Lorre, who also founded the Chuck Lorre Family Foundation, will sit down with Jay Sures, vice chairman of United Talent Agency, for a closing-night discussion on his career and his philanthropic work.

Additional sessions will focus on AI and social media for measuring impact, reality TV as a force for good, faith and media, the podcast-to-feature pipeline, grassroots impact for independent filmmakers, and a hands-on workshop on designing transitional characters with the Sabido Method.

Zcash erased a 13% intraday rally to trade flat at $794 as Grayscale’s leveraged ETF made its NYSE Arca debut on Monday.

Grayscale's new leveraged Zcash ETF fell more than 13% to $28.60 in its first trading session on Monday.

The fund, which trades on NYSE Arca under the ticker ZECX, debuted less than three months after Grayscale's spot ETF began trading.

Grayscale said ZECX is the first 2x leveraged ETF tied to Zcash. It uses swaps to target twice the daily return of its ZCSH spot fund, minus fees and expenses, according to a Monday release.

The firm first offered Zcash exposure through a private placement in 2017 before rolling out ZCSH on NYSE Arca on Aug. 25.

The ZECX fund comes hot on the heels of a Nov. 10 ad campaign from Grayscale that featured a full-page Wall Street Journal wrap and a new educational website focusing on Zcash's privacy features.

"Financial privacy is one of the most important opportunities in crypto, and we believe Zcash is well positioned to lead it," said Kyle Dacruz, Head of Product Development at Grayscale. "ZECX extends our long-standing conviction in Zcash and gives experienced traders a new way to express a short-term bullish view through their brokerage accounts."

Zcash's native ZEC token initially jumped 13% this morning following a surge in equities in crypto tied to Jensen Huang being reinstated as Nvidia's CEO, but by publication had given back all its gains to trade even on the day at $792.

TinyBuild CEO tells devs not to stress about AI clones "because game feel is impossible to copy"

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"As long as you have great game feel and a great game loop, I think you're going to be OK"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The burgeoning AI takeover that ramped up a few years back has caused a lot of human stress for a lot of different reasons. Workers across many industries fear for their livelihoods, and on a much more existential scale, there's been substantial debate from industry leaders about whether AI will one day turn hostile and bring about the extinction of the human species, but that's a topic for another publication. For people who make video games, the frightening advancement of AI capabilities means it's easier for nefarious wannabe game developers to take a legitimate game, toss it into an AI prompt, and come out the other side with a legally distinct copycat.

One way to curb the potential of your game being cloned with AI tools to shorten the time between the game's announcement and release, reckons Alex Nichiporchik, CEO of tinyBuild, the publisher behind the ironically named cyberpunk shopkeeper sim, Probably Stolen. Nichiporchik tells GamesIndustry.biz he thinks "the biggest talking point over the next half year is going to be the time to market, and how devs are now afraid of how AI copies their games."

Much more important than market timing, in Nichiporchik's view, is what he calls "game feel." I probably don't need to explain this, but game feel generally refers to how a game feels to play, who woulda thunk? In other words, it's the tactile sensation of controlling a game on a moment-to-moment level, and it's something generative AI has been shown time and time again to get hideously wrong. Nichiporchik goes a step further and says AI is literally incapable of nailing game feel, and that game developers shouldn't worry about AI clones for that reason.

"Devs should just think about 'How do you make [your game] more difficult to copy?' Because game feel is impossible to copy," he says. "You can have all the references and you can have a lot of outsourcing companies trying to copy your game with proper designers, and they still fail. Why? Because of the game feel.

"As long as you have great game feel and a great game loop, I think you're going to be OK. And the other theory that keeps on confirming itself is time, right? The more time people spend in your game, the more sales you're going to get. So look at your time spent and create great game feel that people love, and everything else is secondary, I think."

Probably Stolen was announced on August 3 and released on October 28, an unusually brief window between reveal and launch, and Nichiporchik says it's already shifted more than a million units. It seems tinyBuild and developer Questing Goose Studio's success has been undeterred by AI clones unable to flood the market in time, or maybe it's the title's unclonable game feel.

Tom Cullen and Joe Locke’s Mystery Thriller ‘Black Church Bay’ Sells to Strand Releasing for North America (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rhys Marc Jones’s “Black Church Bay,” a mystery thriller starring “Heartstopper” breakout Joe Locke and Tom Cullen (“Weekend”), has been bought by Strand Releasing for North America.

Jones’ feature debut, “Black Church Bay” follows a man whose world unravels after a secret relationship ends in tragedy. Set against the backdrop of Anglesey in North Wales, the thriller explores gay repression, identity and the lengths people will go to conceal their true selves. Related Stories Trump Posts Bizarre AI Meme of Jensen Huang Holding Up a Bra, Riffing Off 2024 Assassination Attempt on President, After Nvidia Board Reinstates CEO Billy Porter, Hannah John-Kamen Board 'The Colour of My Skin,' From Venice Winner Anuparna Roy and Activist Amar Singh (EXCLUSIVE)

The deal was negotiated by Strand Releasing’s Jon Gerrans and The Bureau Sales’ Clémentine Hugot. Strand is planning a summer 2027 theatrical release.

“We’re thrilled to be working with The Bureau Sales on this innovative thriller that confronts complicated issues of gay repression and Cullen and Locke’s performances are so beautifully directed by Jones and create a real suspenseful thriller that is satisfying,” said Marcus Hu, co-president of Strand Releasing.

“Black Church Bay” world premiered at Zurich and went to play at Busan and the BFI London Film Festival.

“The film is the culmination of my thematic obsessions – a mystery thriller exploring notions of acceptance, authenticity and repression; how it is often our contradictions that define who we are and what we become,” said Jones.

“Black Church Bay” was shot last year on location in Anglesey and produced by Alex Polunin of Scotland-based Ossian International and Tristan Goligher of London-based The Bureau.

Polunin, who previously produced Jones’ short films “Pale Saint” and “Father of the Bride,” developed the film from its inception.

“One of the things that’s most distinctive and unique about this idea is the way in which it combines Rhys’s thematic and formal interests as a filmmaker – specifically in exploring the queer experience in such a nuanced way, but also through the genre engine of a thriller,” Polunin said.

Goligher, meanwhile, pointed to Jones’ ability to build and sustain suspense while exploring morally complex characters.

“It’s really rare to see short films where a filmmaker can create – and sustain – tension in the way that he did. This script does the same thing,“ Goligher said. “We’ve had a lot of very positive messaging around the gay experience in recent years but it’s also not everyone’s experience. Rhys wrote a very specific story about a very specific person and their experience, and I have no doubt that chimes with many people in the UK and the world.”

“But watching someone deal with a moral dilemma – trying to wrestle with the question of should they, and can they, do the right thing at their own cost – that’s also a very universal dilemma,“ added Goligher.

The film was developed with BBC Film, iFeatures and the Cannes Film Festival’s Cinéfondation Résidence. Financing came from BBC Film, Ffilm Cymru Wales, Screen Scotland and private backers including Los Angeles-based Manzini Films, U.K.-based Elephant Film and 1C6 Films, Kamila Serkebaeva and Brazil-based Daniel Staut.

Strand Releasing’s current slate includes Gregg Araki’s 4K restoration of “Mysterious Skin” and David Pablos’ “On the Road,” which represents Mexico in the Oscar race.

Monza, Pessina: "Orgoglioso di essere il capitano. A Varela ho lasciato io il rigore"

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Successo importante del Monza, che vince 3-0 contro la Fiorentina nel posticipo del lunedì valevole per la 12ª giornata di Serie A, interrompendo una striscia di quattro ko consecutivi e salendo a 10 punti in classifica, appena sopra i viola fermi a 9. Al termine del match deciso dalla doppietta di Varela e dal gol di Robinson, il centrocampista biancorosso Matteo Pessina è intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky Sport.

Partiamo da questa sera: sono passati due anni e qualche giorno dalla tua ultima partita in Serie A, dopo che l'ultima gara disputata quest'anno era stata la finale contro il Catanzaro. Come ci si sente?

"Ci si sente bene quando si gioca, ma soprattutto quando si vince. Ho avuto questo piccolo infortunio, però adesso sto bene e sono contento perché posso aiutare la squadra. Abbiamo un grande allenatore che ci fa giocare bene, siamo tanti giovani e tanti italiani: mi piace stare in questo gruppo".

Siete infatti la squadra più giovane di questo campionato. Hai osservato i tuoi compagni anche da fuori, compreso quel giocatore che oggi ha firmato una doppietta con un gol di tacco nato da un tuo assist perfetto: l'avevate provata in allenamento?

"No, non l'avevamo provata, però ha fatto un movimento da attaccante vero. Quando io stoppo quel pallone, lui mi indica dove andrà e io lo capisco immediatamente, intuendo che la palla può entrare soltanto in quel modo. Avevo già visto un film nella mia testa in cui il pallone poteva finire in rete solo di tacco, perché si è inserito perfettamente tra due giocatori. È un grande calciatore e sta crescendo ancora tanto: gli vogliamo tutti bene perché è davvero un gran lavoratore, arrivato con la mentalità giusta in un campionato difficile come questo. Ci sta aiutando molto, siamo contenti e speriamo cresca ancora".

In conferenza stampa Juric ti ha rivolto uno dei complimenti più belli che un calciatore possa ricevere, affermando che alzi il livello e che esistono gli allenamenti con Pessina e quelli senza Pessina. Quanto gratifica un attestato simile a questo punto della tua carriera, in questa seconda fase della tua esperienza al Monza dopo il lungo infortunio?

"Sì, è sicuramente un complimento gratificante. Il mister mi conosce bene già da tempo e sa quale apporto posso dare alla squadra. Questi elogi mi fanno piacere anche perché credo che lui si riveda un po' in me: da giocatore era così e alzava sempre il livello. Pur essendo due calciatori diversi, lui era sempre aggressivo, teneva alto il ritmo e giocava con grande intensità e forza, quindi penso che anche per questo motivo apprezzi il contributo che do alla squadra".

Vuoi quindi dire che hai lo stesso carattere di Juric?

"Non esattamente, lui è molto più severo".

Da campione d'Europa avresti potuto ambire ancora a club molto importanti, essendo un centrocampista completo capace di abbinare qualità, dedizione e quantità. Perché hai scelto di restare nella tua terra, a Monza, dove sei nato?

"Sicuramente incide il legame con il territorio: io qui sono nato e sono cresciuto nel settore giovanile, poi sono stato un po' in giro e infine sono tornato. Mi è stata affidata la fascia da capitano, quindi per me è un bel progetto che mi rende davvero soddisfatto. Ho avuto l'opportunità di andare in squadre magari più blasonate, che potevano lottare per posizioni più alte o per l'Europa, anche se nella vita non si può mai dire dove potremo arrivare: intanto ci godiamo questa vittoria. Sono molto contento del percorso che stiamo facendo; anche la società ha le idee chiare e sa cosa vuole fare, avendo scelto un allenatore coraggioso e una linea di mercato altrettanto coraggiosa. Siamo tanti giovani e tanti italiani, questo ci fa piacere: sono davvero orgoglioso di essere il capitano di questa squadra".

A proposito del tuo attaccamento al gruppo, abbiamo notato un bel gesto in occasione del calcio di rigore: hai preso tu il pallone in mano, ma poi lo hai ceduto a Varela. Non so quali fossero le gerarchie, ma cos'è successo esattamente? Te lo ha chiesto lui?

"È un'annotazione giusta. Diciamo che il battitore designato potevo essere io, però in questo caso mi ha fatto davvero piacere lasciare il pallone a un ragazzo che si sta giocando anche il titolo di capocannoniere del campionato. Non me lo ha chiesto lui: sono stato io a dirgli spontaneamente di calciarlo, e direi che le cose sono andate anche molto bene".

Colgate-Palmolive vs. McDonald's: Which Consumer Goods Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two consumer goods company giants, one leaning on Walmart and the other on thousands of franchisees, offer very different routes to steady returns.

By Lawrence Rothman, CFA – Updated Nov 23, 2026 at 5:11PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Colgate-Palmolive ›

When market volatility rises, many investors turn toward large and established companies for stability. Choosing between Colgate-Palmolive (CL -0.79%) and McDonald's (MCD +1.15%) requires weighing a household goods leader against a global fast-food powerhouse.

Colgate-Palmolive focuses on essential household items like toothpaste and soap, offering stability through products people buy regardless of the economy. McDonald's dominates the quick-service restaurant industry by leveraging a massive franchise network that generates recurring rent and royalty income. While one provides daily necessities and the other offers affordable convenience, both represent mature businesses that investors often compare for long-term consistency.

Colgate-Palmolive operates as a global leader in segments like oral care, home care, and pet nutrition through brands such as Colgate and Hill's. These essential products are sold in over 200 countries, making the company a prominent name among consumer staples stocks. Investors should note that nearly 11% of its 2025 sales came from Walmart (WMT +1.27%), creating a customer concentration that gives the retailer significant bargaining power over trade discounts.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, revenue reached $21.3 billion, which represents a growth rate of approximately 5.9% compared to the prior 12-month period. The company reported net income of $2.2 billion during this same period as it navigated a complex global environment. This performance reflects top-line expansion across its various geographic regions even as it faces competition from smaller, digital-first brands.

McDonald's utilizes a franchise-heavy business model where roughly 95% of its 45,000 restaurants are managed by independent owners. These franchisees provide the company with steady streams of rent and royalties, which support a high net margin.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, revenue reached $27.8 billion, representing a growth rate of roughly 5.8% over the previous 12-month period. The company recorded net income of $9.0 billion. McDonald's reported a net margin of 32.4% during this time frame, illustrating the profitability inherent in its franchise-led structure.

Colgate-Palmolive faces significant risks from its global footprint, as geopolitical instability in regions like the Middle East can disrupt operations. The company also deals with intense competition from private labels. Additionally, evolving global regulations concerning environmental impact and product safety require ongoing investment to ensure compliance.

McDonald's depends on the financial health of its franchisees to execute its expansion plans, and any broad economic downturn could impact its royalty streams. The business is also susceptible to reputational damage from food safety incidents and recent class action lawsuits regarding product labeling and marketing claims.

McDonald's carries a lower Forward P/E, while Colgate-Palmolive appears more attractive based on its P/S ratio, which compares market value to sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

While neither company is a fast grower, they have generated positive sales and earnings gains. That's positive for mature companies.

Additionally, both have made strong commitments to paying dividends to shareholders. That's an important component of their total returns. In fact, Colgate and McDonald's have increased payments annually for 63 and 50 straight years, respectively. That makes them Dividend Kings, or companies that have raised dividends for at least 50 consecutive years.

Both have sufficient earnings to support dividends, too. Colgate-Palmolive has a payout ratio, or dividends divided by earnings, of 82%. Meanwhile, McDonald's has a 59% payout ratio.

Turning to the dividend yields. McDonald's 2.8% yield surpasses Colgate's 2.2% yield.

The decision comes down to the underlying businesses and dividends. With McDonald's higher dividend yield and asset-light business conducive to free cash flow generation, that stock is my choice over Colgate-Palmolive.

Is Social Media Really the Great Equalizer? Here’s What Hulu’s ‘The Circle’ Host Tinx Has to Say (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I sat down with Christina Najjar to talk alliances, authenticity, and the responsibility of being the “internet’s big sister.”

After a seven-season run on Netflix, The Circle has found a new home on Hulu, and things look very, very different. Rather than “normies,” the cast is now made up of familiar faces from the Walt Disney Company reality TV ecosystem and micro-influencers disguised as civilians. (Even the player with the most modest initial following, Cyrus Irani, had over 10,000 followers at the time his casting was announced. Is it a problem? Not necessarily. But it’s certainly a switch-up from season 1’s social media skeptic Shubham Goel.)

The revival series is also airing in near-real time, with new episodes dropping Tuesday through Thursday and audience voting being used to shape the process. And perhaps the biggest change: stepping into hosting duties in place of comedian and actor Michelle Buteau is SiriusXM’s Christina “Tinx” Najjar, who rose to fame on social media during the COVID-19 pandemic.

As a longtime Circle devotee, I was...curious about what these changes might mean for my favorite competition series. So when I got the chance to fly to Atlanta to visit the set (and meet Tinx) myself, I took it. There I chatted with her about the power of social media, the potential problem of having celebs in the Circle, and the player she thinks might win it all.

I truly couldn’t believe it. I know how many loyal fans The Circle has, so when I got the call that I got it—I mean, I’m still pinching myself every day. It’s a really big moment for me, and I know I have big shoes to fill. And I really just want to make everybody proud.

I remember watching the very first episode and thinking, “Wow, this is such a fresh concept, and it’s truly a social experiment for the modern age.” So the fact that I’m here all these years later is truly a pinch-me moment.

I relate to it so much, because in COVID, I was living in a studio apartment in L.A. I had just moved. I didn’t have a lot of friends, and all I had was my phone. That’s why I initially picked up TikTok and thought, “Who knows what’s going to happen in the world? I might as well make a few videos and try to make friends online.” And as soon as I started creating content, I just realized, “Wow, I love connecting with people. I love entertaining people.” It’s been a wild six years ever since.

I grew up doing theater in high school, but honestly, no. I truthfully had a lot of limiting beliefs about myself. I just thought there was no way I could ever be an influencer. I didn’t feel pretty enough. I didn’t think I was put together enough. And I think one thing COVID taught us is that anybody who has a dream can go for it. Social media is really the great equalizer in that sense, because if you are funny, if you have something to say, you can get online and you can connect with people.

It’s such an honor, and I love that people look up to me for advice. It’s the honor of my life that people trust me enough to call me on my radio show for advice or write to me, and I just try to be a voice of reason and remind women to know their worth. I feel like this is a journey I was meant to be on, and all of the opportunities that being online has afforded me, because it really has given me so much. The fact that I’m sitting here as the host of The Circle is—again, I can’t believe it.

It’s so different because I’m used to just holding up my phone, recording, cutting quickly, and pressing post. This is a totally different operation, but the crew is so cool, production is so cool, and the writers that I work with are just so smart and so funny. I was actually shocked when they explained to me the process of how close to the wire we are. It is as close to real time as you could ever think.

Because I have lived online for six years, I post in real time myself. I’m someone who wakes up and I’m like, “Hey guys, I’m going to the gym. Here’s what I’m eating for breakfast.” I don’t know if I would know how to do it any other way, because I’m just, like, an oversharer, so I think I would just be myself and see what happened. But I can be strategic. I feel like I would make a lot of alliances. I’m good at that.

I honestly think maybe the celebrities are at a slight disadvantage because, as you say, people have perceptions about them. I feel like they also have more of a target on their backs.

He was shocked. He had such a good strategy. I was honestly shocked he got out, too. He’s so likable, and everyone knew it was really him. He got got. He got got by Justin [Hinsley], if I’m being honest, who has very strong strategy. I think Justin is incredibly strategic and knows how to play the game.

I mean, Hannah [Brown], obviously. I don’t think she knows how to play any other way. She’s so genuine, and she’s definitely playing with her heart. So we’ll see how that fares and whether it’s strategy or heart that wins.

I think it’s totally fine, because there’s a lot on the line here. This is a popularity contest, but there’s a big prize at the end. So I think it’s fun when people are ruthless. Why not? It’s a game, and games are meant to be won.

Scan Walmart Clearance With the App – It Can Ring Up Below the Sticker

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

A Walmart clearance sticker keeps the price it was printed with, and the store may have marked the item down again since. Scan the item with the free Walmart app while you’re in the store. The app shows that store’s current price, and that’s what you’ll pay at the register.

Walmart says each store’s manager can cut prices to clear out extra stock, and that changing prices on paper tags by hand could take hours or even days. The register charges the new price in the meantime.

Walmart is replacing its paper shelf tags with digital labels that show the same price the register charges. It said in March 2026 that it expects every U.S. store to have them within a year. A clearance sticker on the item itself is still paper, though, so it’s the likeliest place to find an old price, along with the shelf tags in any store that hasn’t switched yet.

Say a pair of kids’ sneakers carries a $14.97 clearance sticker, and the store has since cut the price to $5. The sticker still says $14.97, but the app shows $5, and $5 is what you’ll pay.

Look for clearance stickers on the displays at the ends of aisles, along the back wall of each department, and in the seasonal aisle the week after a holiday. Open the app inside the store with location sharing on, since it shows in-store prices only once it knows you’re there. Then tap the scanner icon in the search bar and scan the product’s own barcode, not one printed for shipping or storage.

The price you see applies only at that store. Walmart says prices can vary from store to store and that it doesn’t price match its own stores, so the store won’t match a lower price you found at another Walmart. Clearance is also whatever the store has left, so an item you pass up today may be gone the next time you come in.

For a big purchase you can time, like a TV or a mattress, skip the clearance hunt and wait for its category’s yearly sale season.

Scan every clearance sticker before the item goes in your cart, and buy what scans at a price you’d pay.

Download the free Walmart app and sign in before your next trip.

The 101 money moves that matter most, from the foundations to protecting what you’ve built.

Tuya Reports Third Quarter 2026 Unaudited Financial Results and Declaration of Cash Dividend

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SANTA CLARA, Calif., Nov. 23, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Tuya Inc. ("Tuya" or the "Company") (NYSE: TUYA; HKEX: 2391), a global leading AI cloud platform service provider, today announced its unaudited financial results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026 and the declaration of a cash dividend.

For further information on the non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

Mr. Xueji (Jerry) Wang, Founder and Chief Executive Officer of Tuya, commented, "In the third quarter, opportunities and challenges coexisted in the global consumer electronics industry. On the one hand, AI-native hardware continued to move from concept toward deployment at scale. New categories such as companion robots and AI toys rose rapidly, becoming a new engine for industry growth, which fully demonstrated that users' new demands for hardware in the AI era have begun to emerge. On the other hand, upstream component costs such as memory chips were still rising, and demand in traditional categories also faced pressure. Against this backdrop, we still maintained solid growth, with total revenue increasing by 19.5% year over year to US$98.6 million. The growth rate further accelerated compared to the second quarter, and we achieved year-over-year growth for the 13th consecutive quarter. In particular, PaaS revenue grew by 18.3% year over year, reflecting the continued increase in penetration of smart products across different regions and categories.

We continued to fully embrace AI and accelerated the promotion of ecosystem deployment. During the quarter, shipments of new AI-native categories grew by over 400% year over year. We debuted the AI home companion robot Doova at IFA in Berlin and the Global Digital Trade Expo. With the AI life assistant Hey Tuya as a core entry point, we integrated the three major application ecosystems of AI Home, AI Robot, and AI Energy, as well as the full-stack Matter combo solution. At the same time, subscription businesses such as door lock value-added services and pet cloud storage developed steadily, driving a continuous increase in the proportion of recurring revenue. Regarding developer tools, following Tuya Cobuilder, we launched the AI-native no-code application development platform Tuya AI Coding this quarter, extending developer tools from hardware development to application development. Looking ahead, we will focus on the three major scenarios mentioned above to promote large-scale shipments and the commercialization of AI-native hardware such as companion robots, pet care devices, and home energy management devices, and continue to improve the developer toolchain, Agent orchestration, and commercialization capabilities."

Mr. Yi (Alex) Yang, Director and Chief Financial Officer of Tuya, added, "In the third quarter, all three business segments delivered year-over-year revenue growth, with total revenue increasing by 19.5% year over year to US$98.6 million. As a foundation for our growth, the PaaS business saw its revenue increase by 18.3% year over year to US$70.1 million; Premium PaaS customers contributed approximately 89.6% of the PaaS revenue, and the core customer base remained stable. Smart home & robot product revenue grew by 26.7% year over year to US$15.0 million, primarily benefiting from growth in demand for categories such as AI audio and video, AI companion toys, and energy. AI application & others revenue increased by 18.6% year over year to US$13.6 million, primarily driven by the growth in cloud services, AI video cloud storage, and AI value-added services."

Third Quarter 2026 Unaudited Financial Results

Total revenue in the third quarter of 2026 increased by 19.5% to US$98.6 million from US$82.5 million in the same period of 2025.

Total gross profit in the third quarter of 2026 increased by 13.6% to US$45.2 million from US$39.8 million in the same period of 2025. The gross margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 45.8%, compared to 48.3% in the same period of 2025.

Gross margin fluctuated primarily due to changes in product and solution mix, as well as fluctuations in semiconductor supply-chain pricing. As an AI developer platform with a rich ecosystem of smart devices and applications, the Company remains focused on AI offering with compelling value propositions while maintaining economic efficiency.

Operating expenses increased by 0.8% to US$36.3 million in the third quarter of 2026 from US$36.0 million in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP operating expenses increased by 15.9% to US$35.8 million in the third quarter of 2026 from US$30.9 million in the same period of 2025. For further information on the non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

PROFIT FROM OPERATIONS AND OPERATING MARGIN

Profit from operations in the third quarter of 2026 was US$8.9 million, compared to US$3.8 million in the same period of 2025. The Company had a non-GAAP profit from operations of US$9.4 million in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$8.9 million in the same period of 2025, demonstrating consistent operating profitability and leverage.

Operating margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 9.0%, up 4.4 percentage points from 4.6% in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP operating margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 9.5%, down 1.3 percentage points from 10.8% in the same period of 2025.

Net profit in the third quarter of 2026 was US$18.5 million, an increase of 23.8% from US$15.0 million in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP net profit in the third quarter of 2026 was US$19.1 million, compared to US$20.1 million in the same period of 2025.

Net margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 18.8%, an improvement of 0.6 percentage points from 18.2% in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP net margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 19.3%, compared to 24.4% in the same period of 2025.

Basic and diluted net profit per ADS was US$0.03 in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$0.02 in the same period of 2025. Each ADS represents one Class A ordinary share.

Non-GAAP basic and diluted net profit per ADS was US$0.03 in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$0.03 in the same period of 2025.

CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS, TIME DEPOSITS AND TREASURY SECURITIES RECORDED AS SHORT-TERM AND LONG-TERM INVESTMENTS

Cash and cash equivalents, time deposits and treasury securities recorded as short-term and long-term investments were US$971.9 million as of September 30, 2026, compared to US$1,017.3 million as of December 31, 2025. The Company believes its current cash position is sufficient to meet its current liquidity and working capital needs.

NET CASH GENERATED FROM OPERATING ACTIVITIES

Net cash generated from operating activities in the third quarter of 2026 was US$12.7 million, compared to US$30.0 million in the same period of 2025. The net cash generated from operating activities for the third quarter of 2026 was mainly attributable to working capital changes in the ordinary course of business.

For further information on non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

Currently, innovative AI hardware is in a rapid ramp-up phase, but the overall macroeconomic environment remains complex. On the one hand, AI-native hardware is moving from concept toward deployment at scale, with new categories such as companion robots and AI toys rising rapidly, becoming a new engine for industry growth. On the other hand, upstream component costs such as memory chips continue to rise, demand recovery in certain traditional categories is relatively slow, and uncertainties relating to trade policies and supply chains remain. Performance varies across regions, categories and markets. With the accelerated integration of AI and consumer hardware, Physical AI scenarios are ushering in a broader space for development.

Against this backdrop, we will continue to advance our AI-driven strategy. At the product level, we will focus on three high-potential scenarios, namely AI Home, AI Energy and AI Robot, to promote large-scale shipments and the commercialization of AI-native hardware such as companion robots, home energy management devices, and pet care devices. At the developer platform level, we will continue to improve Tuya Cobuilder, Tuya AI Coding and Agent orchestration capabilities, further lowering the development threshold, and enabling more customers and brands to quickly launch their own AI applications based on the Tuya platform. At the user application level, we will continue to enhance the response efficiency and reliability of Hey Tuya, and advance subscription and value-added services such as AI-powered protection, AI energy saving, pet care, and video understanding, to enhance our renewal and ongoing service capabilities.

Looking ahead, we will maintain disciplined execution, and continuously invest in AI R&D and platform capabilities. We will focus on AI-native applications, Physical AI scenarios, and developer platform construction, leveraging the advantages of our platform, device ecosystem and global developer base to transform AI technology from a tool capability into real and scalable commercial value. At the same time, we also recognize that our future performance may be affected by changes in consumer demand, regional economic conditions, inventory cycles, component and logistics costs, foreign exchange and interest-rate volatility, tariffs and trade-policy adjustments, and broader geopolitical uncertainties.

Declaration of Cash Dividend and Record Date

On November 23, 2026, the Board approved the declaration and distribution of a cash dividend (the "Cash Dividend") of US$0.0570 per ordinary share, or US$0.0570 per ADS, to such holders as at the close of business on January 6, 2027, Hong Kong Time and New York Time, respectively. The aggregate amount of the Cash Dividend will be approximately US$35 million, which is payable in U.S. dollars and in cash, and will be funded by surplus cash and to be paid out from the share premium account of the Company. The determination to make distributions and the amount of such distributions will be made at the discretion of its Board and will be based upon the Company's operations and earnings, including, but not limited to, considerations of the Company's GAAP and Non-GAAP net profits, cash flows, financial conditions and other relevant factors.

In order to qualify for the Cash Dividend, with respect to ordinary shares registered on the Company's Hong Kong share register, all valid documents for the transfers of shares accompanied by the relevant share certificates must be lodged with the Company's Hong Kong share registrar, Computershare Hong Kong Investor Services Limited, at Shops 1712-1716, 17th Floor, Hopewell Centre, 183 Queen's Road East, Wan Chai, Hong Kong, no later than 4:30 p.m. on Wednesday, January 6, 2027, Hong Kong time; and with respect to the ordinary shares registered on the Company's principal share register in the Cayman Islands, all valid documents for the transfers of shares accompanied by the relevant share certificates must be lodged with the Company's principal share registrar, Maples Fund Services (Cayman) Limited, at PO Box 1093, Boundary Hall, Cricket Square, Grand Cayman, KY1-1102, Cayman Islands, no later than 3:30 p.m. on Tuesday, January 5, 2027, Cayman Islands time (due to the time difference between Cayman Islands and Hong Kong). Cash Dividend to be paid to the holders of ADSs issued by the depositary of the ADSs will be subject to the terms of the deposit agreement. The payment date is expected to be on or around January 20, 2027 for holders of ordinary shares and on or around January 27, 2027 for holders of ADSs.

Hong Kong Time on Tuesday, November 24, 2026) to discuss the financial results. Tuya Inc. (NYSE: TUYA; HKEX: 2391) is a global leading AI cloud platform service provider with a mission to build an AI developer ecosystem and enable everything to be smart. Tuya has pioneered a purpose-built AI cloud platform with cloud and generative AI capabilities that delivers a full suite of offerings, including Platform-as-a-Service, or PaaS, AI application & others and Smart home & robot products for developers of smart device, commercial applications, and industries. Through its AI developer platform, Tuya has activated a vibrant global developer community of brands, OEMs, AI agents, system integrators and independent software vendors to collectively strive for smart solutions ecosystem embodying the principles of green and low-carbon, security, high efficiency, agility, and openness.

In evaluating the business, the Company considers and uses non-GAAP financial measures, such as non-GAAP operating expenses, non-GAAP profit from operations (including non-GAAP operating margin), non-GAAP net profit (including non-GAAP net margin), and non-GAAP basic and diluted net profit per ADS, as supplemental measures to review and assess its operating performance. The presentation of non-GAAP financial measures is not intended to be considered in isolation or as a substitute for the financial information prepared and presented in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles in the United States of America ("U.S. GAAP"). The Company defines non-GAAP financial measures by excluding the impact of share-based compensation expenses and credit-related impairment/(reversal) of long-term investments from the respective GAAP financial measures. The Company presents the non-GAAP financial measures because they are used by the management to evaluate its operating performance and formulate business plans. The Company also believes that the use of the non-GAAP financial measures facilitates investors' assessment of its operating performance.

Non-GAAP financial measures are not defined under U.S. GAAP and are not presented in accordance with U.S. GAAP. Non-GAAP financial measures have limitations as analytical tools. One of the key limitations of using the aforementioned non-GAAP financial measures is that they do not reflect all items of expenses that affect the Company's operations. Share-based compensation expenses and credit-related impairment/(reversal) of long-term investments have been and may continue to be incurred in the business and are not reflected in the presentation of non-GAAP measures. Further, the non-GAAP financial measures may differ from the non-GAAP information used by other companies, including peer companies, and therefore their comparability may be limited. The Company compensates for these limitations by reconciling the non-GAAP measures to the most directly comparable U.S. GAAP measures, all of which should be considered when evaluating the Company's performance. The Company encourages you to review its financial information in its entirety and not rely on a single financial measure.

Reconciliations of Tuya's non-GAAP financial measures to the most comparable U.S. GAAP measures are included at the end of this press release.

Welcome to elite impunity, Jensen Huang

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

I suppose I must issue a hearty congratulations to Jensen Huang. Huang is CEO of Nvidia again, to the surprise of basically everyone except President Donald Trump and Vice President JD Vance, who made sure it happened.

Now, those of us who had some kind of basic education on what a “free market” is might find it objectionable to have the American president strong-arming a major American company. But our billionaire tech overlords care about the “free market” exactly as much as they care about “free speech,” which is to say not at all. Watching Silicon Valley cheer for what is, effectively, a sign of a command economy has certainly been bracing. But then, why would they give a shit? Their only real beliefs are that they should remain rich and above the law. Jensen may not have exchanged emails with Jeffrey Epstein, but he’s squarely at the Epstein table now.

The deal was reportedly brokered by Peter Thiel’s very own boy

I have argued here, in the past, that the Epstein case laid bare the real politics of the revanchist American right wing: total elite impunity. Well, here it is again! If you are close enough with Trump, it doesn’t matter if you may have jeopardized national security by fucking an alleged Chinese spy. You won’t even be investigated for it before the American president calls in to your board of directors’ meeting to demand you be reinstated. I mean, it is the era of gangster tech regulation — what were any of us expecting? The deal was reportedly brokered by Peter Thiel’s very own boy, Vance.

Just so we can be absolutely clear about what transpired at Nvidia, let’s quickly go through the facts as we know them. A Chinese citizen named Alice Huang acquired the company she renamed Megaspeed, the Singaporean data firm at the center of this inquiry, in 2023. At some point, Huang met Huang, and the two were seen together at a dinner that December. From 2024 to 2025, Megaspeed received more than 200 Nvidia shipments.

In September 2025, the US government began investigating Megaspeed for possible violations of chip export controls; that October, the State Department told Trump of Jensen Huang’s affair with Alice Huang, who was described as a Chinese intelligence asset. Also in October, The New York Times first reported on Megaspeed’s relationship with Nvidia. In December, Trump — who has a close relationship with Jensen Huang — allowed Nvidia to sell more powerful chips to China. In March 2026, Marco Rubio warned the government about Alice, and Trump nonetheless invited Huang to a visit in China. In October, The New York Times reported on the affair. Shortly after Huang sent a note to Nvidia admitting to it, several other reports of infidelity — this time with Nvidia employees — surfaced. On November 12th, Huang resigned. On November 17th, during the Nvidia earnings call, the board’s chair said that the company “does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO.” On November 23rd, Huang was back.

This level of open corruption is genuinely impressive

What changed between last week and this week? A week was not enough time to truly review all the information on Huang and his paramour, Alice “No Relation” Huang, the international woman of mystery. The explanation seems to be that Nvidia’s board succumbed to a pressure campaign from Huang’s fellow billionaires, and a direct intervention from the president and vice president of the United States of America.

Sure, all these CEOs ostensibly wanted Huang back at Nvidia to keep the AI trade going. Still, watching the likes of Epic CEO Tim Sweeney, former Uber CEO Travis Kalanick, investing loudmouth Bill Ackman, and Elon Musk — who actually did appear in the Epstein files, and who seemed to be pretty friendly with Epstein! — pitch a fit last week was instructive. These men worried that they might be punished for wrongdoing, that they did not get big enough to be above the law. They have now been reassured, and I suppose that is what really matters. After all, Trump said on CNBC that he gave Nvidia’s board assurances that Jensen is “absolutely fine.”

This level of open corruption is genuinely impressive, and not just because Huang is CEO of Nvidia again. Almost in passing, Trump noted that he had “protected” Musk in his first term from “a certain incident [that] took place, a very tough incident.” In his own inimitable style, Trump told Jim Cramer, “You wouldn’t have Elon if it wasn’t for me.” Surely there is no possible downside when the CEOs of two of the biggest corporations on Earth owe their jobs to Trump personally.

Surely there is no possible downside when the CEOs of two of the biggest corporations on Earth owe their jobs to Trump personally

One thing that has perhaps emboldened this crew is the stance from professional politics-knowers — and House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries — that holding members of the Trump administration accountable might be a hindrance to governing. That accountability was part of the reason for the blue wave in November seems to have bypassed these pundits (and the presumptive House majority leader). But then, why should we believe that Democrats will thoroughly investigate this? Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer is claiming to want an investigation, but I am skeptical of how deep that investigation will go as long as Schumer leads it, and only partially because he’s been allied with Silicon Valley for more than a decade. He also has a daughter who works at OpenAI, a company that Nvidia invests in and that is dependent on Nvidia chips. How hard is he really willing to go?

Of course, there may be more to this than just pure economic self-interest. In celebrating Huang’s return, Trump posted a remarkable image. It is an altered picture of Trump himself, taken after a failed assassination attempt in 2024. Trump’s head has been replaced with Jensen Huang’s head. The American flag has been replaced with the Nvidia logo. Huang is waving a bright red bra. It seems I owe Sigmund Freud an apology. In the end, these men fear nothing more than losing power for sexual shenanigans — and especially losing power to a woman.

Leave aside the national security issues for a minute and focus on the two Nvidia employees. Dating subordinates is a serious conflict of interest; the CEO of Nestle lost his job for just that last year. This is a major violation for CEOs of normal companies, but it is common practice among the sleazeballs of Silicon Valley: Bill Gates is famous for it and it was plainly rampant at Google, a tone set by Sergey Brin himself. I am sure that you will be shocked, shocked to discover that in the wake of MeToo, Brin was among the men who suddenly swung right. I mean, Musk himself announced he was a Republican the day before Business Insider reported that he exposed himself to a flight attendant and paid hush money to cover it up.

If you’re rich enough, and connected enough, everything is permitted

The Republican Party has made the abuse of women without consequences one of its main issues; that the lesser violation of sleeping with subordinates would also be something the revanchists would defend seems obvious. That was clear from the botched investigation of the Epstein affair itself, the botched release of the Epstein files, and the assorted confirmation hearings of Pete Hegseth, Brett Kavanaugh, and Todd Blanche. (To say nothing of the Andrew Tate business.) But in all this, let’s not forget that Trump has been accused of sexual misconduct by no fewer than 28 women. So I’m sure it was an affront to all these guys that not only did Huang’s misconduct include a series of affairs, but he was also replaced by Colette Kress, who is known to be a woman.

I suppose we might as well pass by the various reports that Huang allegedly terrorized his employees. In light of everything else, this seems minor by comparison.

Anyway I had that all in mind when I saw Epic’s Sweeney celebrating the state intrusion into theoretically private companies by posting “Back in Black,” an oblique reference to Huang’s leather jackets that is also from the later, lesser period of AC/DC, after Bon Scott died and AC/DC became an AC/DC tribute band. He also called The New York Times’ reporting into Huang “lies.” Musk continued his streak of Sephiroth posting: “The mistake has been corrected.” Jason Calacanis, the All-In podcast’s village idiot, posted, “GPU Jesus has risen!” along with an openly blasphemous, AI-generated image of Huang as Christ.

Best of luck to Joon Kim, the Wachtell Lipton partner and former federal prosecutor who is conducting “a thorough and independent investigation” into Huang’s alleged misconduct on behalf of Nvidia’s board. I assume Kim has, like the rest of us, gotten the message loud and clear: If you’re rich enough, and connected enough, everything is permitted. Or perhaps, more succinctly: lol nothing matters.

Monza, Varela: "Il gol di tacco? È istinto. Non penso alle voci di mercato, ringrazio solo il club"

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Al termine del netto successo per 3-0 conquistato dal Monza contro la Fiorentina, l'attaccante biancorosso Gustavo Varela ha commentato così la sua prestazione ai microfoni di Sky Sport.

Ci insegni a segnare un gol di tacco come quello che hai fatto stasera?

"No, è stato puro istinto, non è qualcosa che provo in allenamento. È arrivato un cross molto bello, ero lì vicino alla porta e ho deciso di provarci: se non ci provi non puoi farcela, ed è andata così".

Anche oggi hai messo a segno una doppietta, raggiungendo quota undici reti e tagliando il traguardo della doppia cifra.

"Continuo a ringraziare il Monza per avermi permesso di essere qui. È stato tutto pazzesco fino a questo momento: mi sento molto fortunato a far parte di questa realtà e spero soltanto di poter continuare su questa strada".

In Italia si parla già molto di te in chiave calciomercato: cosa ne pensi di tutte queste voci?

"È normale, fa parte del calcio. Oggi sto vivendo un grande momento, mentre domani potrebbe arrivarne uno negativo. Per questo cerco semplicemente di godermi il presente senza prestare attenzione alle notizie: a volte si parla bene e altre volte si parla male, ma questo è il mondo del football e io resto concentrato solo su quello che devo fare in campo".

Questa facilità nel trovare la porta fa parte del tuo DNA naturale?

"Penso di aver preso tutte le qualità da mio padre. Lui giocava come difensore centrale, quindi è stato proprio lui a insegnarmi come fare gol".

How To Watch Fjord At Home

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cristian Mungiu's Palme d'Or-winning, Sebastian Stan-starring film "Fjord" will be available to rent on digital platforms beginning November 24, 2026. Given that this is a few days before Thanksgiving, "Fjord" will be the perfect film to watch with the extended family, and won't inspire any kind of snippy, angry political discussions whatsoever.

That, of course, is sarcasm. "Fjord" is, in fact, very, very political. According to /Film's review, "Fjord" is a clumsy, one-sided, and very ideologically weighted drama that is presented as unbiased through its efficient visuals and realistic style, but which is very biased indeed. Even in a room of people who are politically aligned, either to the left or the right, "Fjord" will inspire discussion. Or an argument. Watch it with your politically opposed family members, and let the sparks fly.

"Fjord" tells the story of the Gheorghiu family, which consists of stern Romanian father Mihai (not listed as one of Stan's best roles on /Film's countdown of them) and Norwegian mother Lisbet (Renate Reinsve), along with their two teens, two elementary-school-age kids, and an infant, who move from their long-term Romanian home to the small Norwegian town where Lisbet grew up. The Gheorghius are conservative Christians, which is very different from the local community, mostly made up of non-adherents.

When the Gheorghiu parents are unexpectedly accused of physically abusing their children, Norwegian Child Protective Services immediately takes the kids away (like, in an afternoon), leaving the Gheorghius having to find any kind of legal recourse to get them back. They will eventually have to explain themselves in court. The Gheorghius aren't wholly innocent, confessing to spanking their kids from time to time.

The progressive government of Norway is presented as being full of harridans and busybodies who are hellbent on removing children from their well-meaning parents. "Fjord" presents a political conflict between conservative and liberal ideologies. The Gheorghius attempt to gin up support for their cause by calling in conservative protestors and staging their legal struggle as a bias against Christians. By Romanian social norms, a light spanking is seen as socially appropriate, but the Norwegian government is determined to keep the Gheorghiu kids away from Mihai and Lisbet. The movie sides with the conservative characters, making the entire film feel like light social propaganda.

"Fjord," however, won the Palme d'Or at Cannes, as well as the François Chalais Prize, the FIPRESCI Prize, the Prize of the Ecumenical Jury, and the Prix de la Citoyenneté. It is also beloved by most critics, racking up a 91% approval rating on Rotten Tomatoes, based on 165 reviews. Justin Chang's review in The New Yorker noted that some of the villains in "Fjord" are unrealistically larger-than-life, but that it was ultimately subtle. He wrote:

"Is 'Fjord' guilty, as some have charged, of stacking the moral decks in the Gheorghius' favor? We are presented with a few potentially hissable villains ... Yet Mungiu has an uncommon ability to temper bluntness with nuance."

As soon as it's easily available to rent online, one can watch "Fjord" for themselves and see if it's politically charged, naturally neutral, or morally complex. Cristian Mungiu tends to handle politically difficult issues with frankness and directness; he reminded us of the necessity of abortion with his 2007 drama "4 Months, 3 Weeks and 2 Days." You, however, are the one can must decide if "Fjord" is as powerful.

So Many Dyson Hair Tools Are on Sale Right Now—These Are <i><em>Cosmo</em></i> Editors’ 6 Favorites

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

4 beauty editors. 6 Dyson tools. Here’s our official ranking.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

As we haircare lovers all know, Dyson has truly solidified itself as the crème de la crème of buzzy hair tools. From the viral Airwrap i.d. to the OG Supersonic Hair Dryer, Dyson = high-tech hairstyling. Despite the higher price points, these tools are everywhere these days, from your favorite creator doing their hair on your TikTok feed to your hairstylist doing your hair in the salon chair. It seems like everyone loves a Dyson product (and that includes pretty much every Cosmo editor too). They’ve won tonssss of Cosmo beauty awards, including a 2025 Holy Grail Beauty Award and 2026 Reader’s Choice Beauty Award for the Airwrap, back-to-back wins in the Reader’s Choice Beauty Awards in 2024 and 2025 for the Supersonic, and many, many more.

However, at $250-$500 a pop, it’s not like most people can afford to own every single Dyson tool out there. So which ones are the most worth the hundreds of dollars they cost? Luckily, we’ve here at Cosmo have tested all of ‘em—these are the ones we truly recommend spending money on.

Why we love it: Despite so many Airwrap dupes out there, us Cosmo editors keep going back to the original Dyson hair styling tool that changed the game forever. Talk about true innovation—the Airwrap created a whole new category of styling products that dry, curl, volumize, straighten, and blow out hair all in one handy tool that, oh by the way, uses hot air instead of high heat that could potentially damage your hair more.

It’s also on rare sale right now—23 percent off—for Black Friday and Cyber Monday, so if you’ve been thinking of scooping one up, this is your time to shine. Run, don’t walk!

One Cosmo editor says: “If the Airwrap has zero fans, I’m dead. For years, I didn’t know how to style my hair. The round-brush-and-dryer combo was a lot for me, I didn’t know how to curl my hair, and quite honestly, all I ever did was straighten it with a flat iron (which was perfect for the mid-2010s style at the time). But when I got my hands on my first Airwrap, that all changed. Suddenly, I could do it all, and I was imparting wayyy less heat damage onto my strands than if I had been using traditional hot tools. The i.d. is great because the it has all the attachments I need, it dries and styles my thick, wavy hair super quickly, it eliminates frizz, and keeps my style in place all day.” —CATHARINE MALZAHN, CONTRIBUTING BEAUTY WRITER

Why we love it: The one that started it all and turned hair dryers from a humble styling tool into a must-have item... Dyson’s Supersonic dryer launched 10 years ago in 2016, yet it still remains one of the best hair dryers out there. And honestly, it’s not hard to see why. Beyond the basics you’d be looking for (long cord, negative ions, multiple heat and speed settings, relatively lightweight, that kind of thing), its powerful airflow dries hair noticeably quickly while the dryer monitors the temperature 40 times a second to prevent extreme heat on your strands.

It’s pretty straightforward and easy to use, making a salon-worthy blowout at home feel a whole lot less intimidating. A decade later, the Supersonic is proof that some beauty investments really do stand the test of time.

One Cosmo editor says: “This is the first Dyson hair tool I ever used, and I still think it's probably the most worth-it tool price-wise if you wanted to splurge on one thing. I used to hate blow dryers until I tried this one. It's quick and powerful without sacrificing the health of my hair. This fully dries my hair in under two minutes (yes, two), but it doesn't leave my hair crunchy or fried either.” —BETH GILLETTE, BEAUTY EDITOR

Why we love it: Straightening without hot plates? And you can use it on wet hair? Sign me up. The Dyson Airstrait uses airflow to dry and straighten hair at the same time, meaning you can skip the blow-dryer-and-flat-iron combo entirely. Perfect if you’re trying to cut down on styling time in the morning!

Unlike traditional flat irons, the Airstrait doesn’t use heated plates. Instead, it uses airflow to smooth and dry hair, delivering a soft, naturally straight finish with movement rather than that super-flat, pin-straight look. Plus, it has a dry mode for touching up your style between washes. When our editors with varying curl patterns (from wavy and fine to 4b curls) tested it, it straightened with ease and speed.

One Cosmo editor says: “I have an interesting hair type because while it’s curly and fine, I have a lot of it. It can take a very long time for me to dry my hair, but the Airstrait cut my styling time dramatically. This tool takes my hair from damp to fully dry in under 10 minutes. However, it did take a slight learning curve at first.” —MARY HONKUS, CONTRIBUTING BEAUTY WRITER

Why we love it: Meet the Dyson hair dryer that had three Cosmo beauty editors with three different hair types completely obsessed. The Dyson Supersonic r takes everything we love about the original Supersonic and makes it lighter, smaller, and easier to maneuver, thanks to its curved, ergonomic design. In fact, it’s so lightweight that it weighs less than a pound, making it perfect for professional stylists as well as anyone who doesn’t want an arm workout just to dry hair.

Speaking of which, your arms probably won’t get super tired because of how quickly it works. Beauty director Lauren Balsamo said it dried her soaking-wet hair in just six minutes(!!!). Add in the intelligent attachments that automatically adjust heat and airflow and remember those settings for the next time you use it, the multiple attachments, and the compact size, and it’s easy to be willing to overlook the hefty price tag.

One Cosmo editor says: “The first thing I noticed when I picked up the Supersonic R was the weight, or rather, the lack thereof. It seriously feels like you're holding a bottle of hairspray—that’s how airy and easy it is to handle. Then came the assembly. The magnetic attachments snapped on effortlessly. And as a wavy-haired girl who can either lean curly or reach for a round brush for a bouncy blowout, I really appreciated the range of attachments for everything from creating curls to smoothing flyaways.” —JASMINE HYMAN, ASSISTANT BEAUTY EDITOR

Why we love it: Dyson finally made a one-step blow dryer brush, and at $250, it’s the brand’s lowest-priced hair tool (though it’s still an investment!). Like the Airwrap, the Dyson Airsmooth is another wet-to-dry styler, using powerful airflow to dry and smooth strands without extreme heat. Its teardrop shape helps you get closer to the root for more lift and volume, while the bristles are designed to minimize snagging, pulling, or breakage. It can smooth thick, wavy hair in under 15 minutes, leaving it sleek, shiny, and noticeably less frizzy.

If you’re shopping for a new blow dryer brush, this is a great pick. It’s perfect for anyone who loves a smooth, bouncy blowout but doesn’t want all the bells and whistles of an Airwrap.

One Cosmo editor says: “I was thrilled to find out Dyson had created a one-stop tool for drying and styling, complete with upgraded bristle technology. I want to preserve as many of my precious strands as possible, and what I love most about this tool (besides the low-for-Dyson price) is that its bristles aren’t your basic plastic teeth that tug at your hair. Instead, they bend with your strands as you pull the brush through, releasing them rather than fighting against the tension. I usually finish styling with a solid clump of hair left behind on my tool, but with the Airsmooth, there were truly fewer than five strands caught in the bristles.” —JH

Why we love it: An update from the iconic and original Supersonic, this even-more-high-tech dryer features a Scalp Protect mode that uses sensors to detect how close the tool is to your head and automatically adjusts the heat to help maintain a scalp temperature of approximately 131°F. Translation: You can dry your roots without blasting your scalp with so much heat that you flinch when your dryer gets too close.

It also features intelligent attachments that remember your preferred settings, plus a Pause Detect function that reduces airflow when you put the dryer down. And, of course, you still get the powerful, fast-drying performance Dyson is known for.

One Cosmo editor says: “I have the Supersonic at my parents' house (I gifted my old one to my mom a few years ago), but the Nural is at mine. This is the more high-tech version of the Supersonic, which automatically shifts air flow and heat based on how close the tool is to your scalp. If blow dryers often feel like they're burning off your scalp, this one will turn that around.” —BG

Dyson hair tools are definitely not one-size-fits-all, and what works wonders on someone’s fine, straight hair might not do the same for thick curls or coils.

Before dropping hundreds of dollars on a Dyson, ask yourself: What do I actually want this thing to do? If you’re constantly switching up your hairstyle, the Airwrap’s versatility might justify the splurge. But if your goal is simply to dry your hair quickly, you probably don't need all those attachments—a Supersonic will do the trick. Love a sleek, straight finish? Try the Airstrait. Simply consider which tools you’d actually see yourself using every day before taking the plunge.

Short answer: Heck yeah! There’s a reason Dyson hair tools have earned cult-favorite status among us beauty editors, hairstylists, and pretty much everyone on TikTok. We’ve tested practically every Dyson out there, and we’re consistently impressed. For thick, long, or doesn’t-want-to-stay-styled hair, these can be especially worth the money.

Of course, there’s no denying that they’re a serious investment. So if you’re looking for a budget alternative, there are plenty of options out there too. But in our experience, the Dyson’s quick-drying capabilities sans excessive heat damage, customizable settings, and long-lasting results make them worth the splurge—especially if you’re someone who styles their hair regularly. And while you certainly don't need to own every Dyson tool (your bank account can thank us later), finding the right one for your hair type and styling goals can be a total game changer.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s written about all things hair, from cool hair color trends to the best haircuts worth trying. She’s an avid Airwrap user and has tested several versions of the hair styler over the years.

Anthropic launches Claude Ship, letting AI agents review, fix, and approve code changes

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic is moving from having Claude write code to helping developers decide when that code is ready to ship. On Monday, the newly public AI company announced the launch of Claude Ship, a suite of AI agents for automating code reviews, fixes, and approvals of routine code changes.

The latter is the more interesting part of this new set of tools, as AI coding tools already offered code review features before now. What’s notable about Claude Ship is not just that it can review proposed code changes and identify and address problems, but that it also includes an agent that can approve code changes automatically based on policies set by a development team.

The launch arrives at a time when AI coding agents have become increasingly capable of generating substantial amounts of code, leading to bottlenecks in reviewing and approving the code changes before they go live. With Claude Ship, the idea is to automate even more of the work involved in the development process, essentially by delegating approval authority to an AI agent itself.

“Most teams want a person to approve every change, and that’s easy to keep up with when people write every line. But a lot of developers build with agents now,” a company blog post explains. “On the Claude Code team, Claude writes most of our code, and we wanted everything after the PR to move that fast too.”

Claude Ship is launching in beta, allowing early adopters to use it to review pull requests (PRs), and then decide what’s ready to merge.

The new offering consists of Code Review, which is already generally available, and two new agents now in beta: PR Steward and Claude Approvals. PR Steward focuses on addressing issues such as failing CI checks, merge conflicts, and feedback from Code Review. It also comments after every push on what it changed and how to roll it back, if needed.

Claude Approvals then applies an approval policy that the team writes as a file in the repository. This approval process can incorporate various signals, including Code Review findings or the company’s own classifiers, with policies written in natural language.

Because the offering is still in beta, Anthropic suggests running the approval functionality in observe-only mode first. Teams can also choose to start with just one repository and set monthly spending limits to have more control over the process.

Anthropic says teams will also still remain in control of where Claude Ship runs, which review settings to use (e.g., Quick, Standard, or Deep), and which changes should be escalated to a human for a decision.

Claude Ship is rolling out to Claude Team and Enterprise plans for repositories on GitHub. Code Review is generally available, while PR Steward and Claude Approvals are in beta.

Ford Has A Dedicated Battery Abuse Team. It Obsesses Over How Cells Fail

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Electric vehicle batteries live a harder life than the ones in your phone or laptop. They spend most of their time outdoors, deal with extreme heat and cold, and can take a beating from road debris and potholes.

That means for battery makers, engineering for durability is a priority right up there with energy density and charging speed. And to make sure its cells can handle the abuse of daily life as an EV, Ford has a team dedicated to understanding how batteries fail—and how to stop it.

“That team is 100% focused on battery abuse, design, and testing as a function,” Wesley Thibault, the global chief engineer of battery product development at Ford said last week during a media roundtable at the automaker’s new battery plant in Marshall, Michigan.

Ford is new to the EV battery business. Last week, it started cranking out lithium iron phosphate (LFP) battery packs at BlueOval Battery Park Michigan, using technology licensed from China’s CATL, the world’s largest battery maker. The batteries will power the all-important Ford Fathom pickup, which the automaker says will enter production toward the end of next year and kick off a new generation of vastly better and more profitable EVs.

Despite the licensing deal, Ford officials said they were involved in developing the batteries from the outset, with a focus on durability.

The job is not as simple as it sounds. The battery abuse team isn’t tasked with destroying fully made cells and then finding ways to make them indestructible. Instead, Thibault said, it is involved in the design process from the start.

“They want to understand down to the physics level, how a cell fails and why, and when it does fail, how we can prevent it from creating harm to the occupants,” he said.

Quality operator at Ford's BlueOval Battery Park Michigan.

For example, the team measures how cells vent, a crucial process that keeps batteries from catching fire or damaging themselves when they overheat. When temperatures inside a cell get too high, the electrolyte, the liquid chemical that shuttles electrons between the anode and cathode, gets hot and releases gases.

If those gases stay trapped, they can cause short circuits or thermal runaway, so they need to escape in a controlled way. Thibault said his team can measure all of this analytically—using computer models—before the cells are validated.

Production adds another layer of scrutiny. At various stages, the materials inside the cell, as well as the finished cell itself, go through thousands of quality checks, Ford engineers said. Those include CT scans, X-ray machines, lasers, camera inspections, and AI systems.

For example, when the anode slurry is coated onto a copper foil, every millimeter is scanned by lasers for wrinkles and other anomalies. All employees have the ability to pause production if they spot any problems.In case of any issues, they quarantine the cells affected during a specific time period. Ford also claimed that impacted components can be traced all the way from production to the vehicle they end up in.

To be sure, not all of this is unique to Ford. Strict quality checks like these are common among major battery makers, who all aim to minimize scrap rates for costly batteries and maximize the amount of material that actually gets sold.

Ford engineer operating a winding machine, which brings together the anode and cathode, adding a separator in between.

Ford continuously tests the manufacturing process in other ways, too. Engineers said they intentionally feed damaged components and cells—which they call “red rabbits”—into the production machinery to confirm the quality systems catch them and are working as intended.

When cells are almost ready, they’re blasted with helium gas to check for leaks. Once a pack is assembled, it’s mounted on a hydraulic shaker that mimics thousands of miles and years of real-world road use. The shaker can twist, dive, squat, contort, yaw, and replicate the other movements of a car, putting the pack through everything it would see on the road.

Ford’s EV batteries have fared well, by and large. High-mileage Mach-Es with 100,000 or even 200,000 miles on the odometer show little range loss compared to their original capacity, according to data the company released this month.

On the other hand, Ford’s emphasis on battery quality isn’t exactly surprising. The company has grappled with high warranty costs in recent years. And both the Mustang Mach-E and the F-150 Lightning have had issues linked to their high-voltage batteries. Some early Mach-Es were recalled because their battery contactors would overheat during DC fast-charging. A small number of Lightnings were recalled in 2023 due to defective battery cells.

Ford will want to avoid any similar quality issues as it rolls out the high-stakes Fathom, which it hopes will outsell its previous EVs and set the stage for an electric comeback. It will be years before we know how the Fathom's batteries hold up in the real world, but if the testing so far is any indication, the early signs look promising.
